The film told the story of the musical group [The Four Seasons](/wiki/The_Four_Seasons_\(band\) "The Four Seasons \(band\)").[261] Eastwood directed _[American Sniper](/wiki/American_Sniper "American Sniper")_ (also 2014), a film adaptation of [Chris Kyle](/wiki/Chris_Kyle "Chris Kyle")'s eponymous memoir, following Steven Spielberg's departure from the project.[262] Released on Christmas Day, _American Sniper_ grossed more than $350 million domestically and over $547 million globally, making it one of Eastwood's biggest movies commercially.[263][264] His next film, _[Sully](/wiki/Sully_\(film\) "Sully \(film\)")_ (2016), starred [Tom Hanks](/wiki/Tom_Hanks "Tom Hanks") as [Chesley Sullenberger](/wiki/Chesley_Sullenberger "Chesley Sullenberger"), who successfully landed the [US Airways Flight 1549](/wiki/US_Airways_Flight_1549 "US Airways Flight 1549") on the [Hudson River](/wiki/Hudson_River "Hudson River") in an emergency landing, keeping all passengers on board alive.[265] It became another commercial success for Eastwood, grossing over $238 million worldwide.[266] He directed the biographical thriller _[The 15:17 to Paris](/wiki/The_15:17_to_Paris "The 15:17 to Paris")_ (2018), which saw previously non-professional actors [Spencer Stone](/wiki/Spencer_Stone "Spencer Stone"), [Anthony Sadler](/wiki/Anthony_Sadler "Anthony Sadler"), and [Alek Skarlatos](/wiki/Alek_Skarlatos "Alek Skarlatos") playing themselves as they stop the [2015 Thalys train attack](/wiki/2015_Thalys_train_attack "2015 Thalys train attack").[267] The film received a generally negative reception from critics, who were largely critical of the acting by the three leads.[268] Eastwood next starred in and directed _[The Mule](/wiki/The_Mule_\(2018_film\) "The Mule \(2018 film\)")_ , which was released in December 2018. He played Earl Stone, an elderly drug smuggler based on [Leo Sharp](/wiki/Leo_Sharp "Leo Sharp"), Eastwood's first acting role since _Trouble with the Curve_ in 2012.[269] On the eve of _The Mule_ 's opening, news belatedly surfaced that Eastwood's ex-significant other Sondra Locke had died over a month earlier on November 3; no explanation was given for the [media blackout](/wiki/Media_blackout "Media blackout").[270] Given Locke's extensive history of deceiving the public, scholars deduced that she must have requested the blackout in her final wishes, so as to prevent her true age from being broadcast to the world.[130]

In May 2019, it was announced that Eastwood would direct _The Ballad of Richard Jewell_ , based on the life of heroic security guard [Richard Jewell](/wiki/Richard_Jewell "Richard Jewell"), who was wrongly suspected in the [1996 Olympic bombing](/wiki/1996_Olympic_bombing "1996 Olympic bombing"). Later retitled simply _[Richard Jewell](/wiki/Richard_Jewell_\(film\) "Richard Jewell \(film\)")_ , Eastwood directed and produced the film, through Warner Bros., his tenth straight film with the company. [Jonah Hill](/wiki/Jonah_Hill "Jonah Hill") and Leonardo DiCaprio were originally set to star in the film in 2014, when it was to be directed by [Paul Greengrass](/wiki/Paul_Greengrass "Paul Greengrass"),[271] but DiCaprio and Hill would ultimately serve only as producers on Eastwood's film.[272][273] The film stars [Paul Walter Hauser](/wiki/Paul_Walter_Hauser "Paul Walter Hauser") in the titular role, along with [Sam Rockwell](/wiki/Sam_Rockwell "Sam Rockwell"), [Kathy Bates](/wiki/Kathy_Bates "Kathy Bates"), [Jon Hamm](/wiki/Jon_Hamm "Jon Hamm"), and [Olivia Wilde](/wiki/Olivia_Wilde "Olivia Wilde") in supporting roles. Filming began on June 24, 2019, and _Richard Jewell_ was released on December 13, 2019. 

In October 2020, it was announced that Eastwood would direct, produce, and star in _[Cry Macho](/wiki/Cry_Macho_\(film\) "Cry Macho \(film\)")_ , an adaptation of [the 1975 novel of the same name](/wiki/Cry_Macho "Cry Macho"), for Warner Bros. Pictures.[274] Production of the film took place in New Mexico between November and December 2020.[275] It was released on September 17, 2021,[276] to mixed reviews and [commercial failure](/wiki/Box-office_bomb "Box-office bomb"). 

In April 2023, reports emerged that Eastwood would direct and produce _[Juror #2](/wiki/Juror_No._2 "Juror No. 2")_ , from a screenplay by Jonathan Abrams.[277] The film stars [Nicholas Hoult](/wiki/Nicholas_Hoult "Nicholas Hoult"), [Toni Collette](/wiki/Toni_Collette "Toni Collette"), [Zoey Deutch](/wiki/Zoey_Deutch "Zoey Deutch"), [Kiefer Sutherland](/wiki/Kiefer_Sutherland "Kiefer Sutherland"), and [J.K. Simmons](/wiki/J.K._Simmons "J.K. Simmons").[278] Production began in June 2023, but was temporarily suspended due to the [2023 SAG-AFTRA strike](/wiki/2023_SAG-AFTRA_strike "2023 SAG-AFTRA strike"), resuming in November 2023.[279][280] The film was distributed by Warner Bros. and released in November 2024, to generally favorable reviews.[281] It has been rumored that _Juror #2_ may be Eastwood's final directorial effort, though insiders deny this.[282]

## Directorial style

[](/wiki/File:Gran_Torino_Shooting_\(4298193376\).jpg)On the set of _Gran Torino_ , 2008

Beginning with the thriller _Play Misty for Me_ , Eastwood has directed over 30 films, including Westerns, action films, musicals and dramas. He is one of few top Hollywood actors to have also become a critically and commercially successful director. _[The New Yorker](/wiki/The_New_Yorker "The New Yorker")_ 's [David Denby](/wiki/David_Denby "David Denby") wrote that, unlike Eastwood,[283]

> John Ford appeared in just a few silent films; Howard Hawks never acted in movies. Clark Gable, Gary Cooper, Spencer Tracy, James Stewart, Cary Grant, Humphrey Bogart, William Holden, Steve McQueen, and Sean Connery never directed a feature. John Wayne directed only twice, and badly; ditto Burt Lancaster. Paul Newman, Jack Nicholson, Warren Beatty, Robert Redford, Robert De Niro, and Sean Penn have directed a few movies each, with mixed commercial and artistic success.

From the very early days of his career, Eastwood was frustrated by directors' insistence that scenes be re-shot multiple times and perfected, and when he began directing in 1970, he made a conscious attempt to avoid any aspects of directing he had been indifferent to as an actor. As a result, Eastwood is renowned for his efficient film directing and ability to reduce filming time and control budgets. He usually avoids actors' rehearsing and prefers to complete most scenes on the first take.[284][285] Eastwood's rapid filmmaking practices have been compared to those of [Woody Allen](/wiki/Woody_Allen "Woody Allen"), [Ingmar Bergman](/wiki/Ingmar_Bergman "Ingmar Bergman"), and [Jean-Luc Godard](/wiki/Jean-Luc_Godard "Jean-Luc Godard").[_[by whom?](/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Words_to_watch#Unsupported_attributions "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Words to watch")_] When acting in others' films, he has sometimes taken over directing, such as for _The Outlaw Josey Wales_ , if he believes production is too slow.[283] In preparation for filming Eastwood rarely uses [storyboards](/wiki/Storyboard "Storyboard") for developing the layout of a shooting schedule.[286][287][288] He also attempts to reduce script background details on characters to allow the audience to become more involved in the film,[289] considering their imagination a requirement for a film that connects with viewers.[289][290] Eastwood has indicated that he lays out a film's plot to provide the audience with necessary details, but not "so much that it insults their intelligence".[291]

According to _[Life](/wiki/Life_\(magazine\) "Life \(magazine\)")_ magazine, "Eastwood's style is to shoot first and act afterward. He etches his characters virtually without words. He has developed the art of underplaying to the point that anyone around him who so much as flinches looks hammily histrionic."[292] Interviewers Richard Thompson and Tim Hunter commented that Eastwood's films are "superbly paced: unhurried; cool; and [give] a strong sense of real time, regardless of the speed of the narrative",[293] while Ric Gentry considers Eastwood's pacing "unrushed and relaxed".[294] Eastwood is fond of low-key lighting and back-lighting to give his films a "[noir-ish](/wiki/Film_noir "Film noir")" feel.[285][295]

Eastwood's frequent exploration of ethical values has drawn the attention of scholars, who have researched Eastwood's work from ethical and theological perspectives, including his portrayal of justice, mercy, suicide and the angel of death.[296]

## Politics

Main article: [Political life of Clint Eastwood](/wiki/Political_life_of_Clint_Eastwood "Political life of Clint Eastwood")

[](/wiki/File:Ronald_Reagan_and_Clint_Eastwood.jpg)Eastwood with President [Ronald Reagan](/wiki/Ronald_Reagan "Ronald Reagan") in 1987

Eastwood is a former [Republican](/wiki/Republican_Party_\(United_States\) "Republican Party \(United States\)") who has sometimes supported Democrats, and has long shown an interest in [California politics](/wiki/California_politics "California politics"); he is currently a registered [Libertarian](/wiki/Libertarian_Party_\(United_States\) "Libertarian Party \(United States\)").[297]

He won election as the nonpartisan mayor of Carmel-by-the-Sea, California, in April 1986. He earned $200 per month in that position[298] which he donated to the Carmel Youth Center. While in office, he helped to make ice cream legal to consume on city streets,[299] added public restrooms to the public beach, and a city library annex building was built.[298] He served for two years and declined to run for a second term. In 2001, Governor [Gray Davis](/wiki/Gray_Davis "Gray Davis") appointed him to the California State Park and Recreation Commission, and Governor [Arnold Schwarzenegger](/wiki/Arnold_Schwarzenegger "Arnold Schwarzenegger") re-appointed him to the commission in 2009.[300]

Eastwood endorsed [Mitt Romney](/wiki/Mitt_Romney "Mitt Romney") in the [2012 presidential election](/wiki/2012_United_States_presidential_election "2012 United States presidential election").[301] He delivered a primetime address at the [2012 Republican National Convention](/wiki/2012_Republican_National_Convention "2012 Republican National Convention"), where he drew attention for [a speech](/wiki/Clint_Eastwood_at_the_2012_Republican_National_Convention "Clint Eastwood at the 2012 Republican National Convention") he delivered to an empty chair representing President [Barack Obama](/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama"), which he later regretted.[302] On February 22, 2020, Eastwood announced that he would be endorsing Democrat [Michael Bloomberg](/wiki/Michael_Bloomberg "Michael Bloomberg") in the [2020 presidential election](/wiki/2020_United_States_presidential_election "2020 United States presidential election"). Eastwood stated that he wishes that Trump would act "in a more genteel way, without tweeting and calling people names. I would personally like for him to not bring himself to that level."[303]

## Musical interests

See also: [Clint Eastwood discography](/wiki/Clint_Eastwood_discography "Clint Eastwood discography")

Eastwood is an aficionado of [jazz](/wiki/Jazz "Jazz")—particularly [bebop](/wiki/Bebop "Bebop")—and [blues](/wiki/Blues "Blues"), [country and western](/wiki/Country_music "Country music") and classical music. He dabbled in music early on by developing as a boogie-woogie pianist and had originally intended to pursue a career in music by studying for a music theory degree after graduating from high school.[304] In late 1959, Eastwood produced the album _Cowboy Favorites_ , released on the [Cameo](/wiki/Cameo-Parkway_Records "Cameo-Parkway Records") label,[304] which included some classics such as [Bob Wills](/wiki/Bob_Wills "Bob Wills")'s "[San Antonio Rose](/wiki/New_San_Antonio_Rose "New San Antonio Rose")" and [Cole Porter](/wiki/Cole_Porter "Cole Porter")'s "[Don't Fence Me In](/wiki/Don%27t_Fence_Me_In_\(song\) "Don't Fence Me In \(song\)")". Despite his attempts to plug the album by going on a tour, it never reached the [_Billboard_ Hot 100](/wiki/Billboard_Hot_100 "Billboard Hot 100").[304]

In 1963, Cameo producer [Kal Mann](/wiki/Kal_Mann "Kal Mann") told him that "he would never make it big as a singer".[305] Nevertheless, during the off season of filming _Rawhide_ , Eastwood and [Paul Brinegar](/wiki/Paul_Brinegar "Paul Brinegar") - sometimes joined by [Sheb Wooley](/wiki/Sheb_Wooley "Sheb Wooley") - toured rodeos, state fairs, and festivals. In 1962, their act, entitled Amusement Business Cavalcade of Fairs, earned them as much as $15,000 a performance.[305] Although he never made it as a major performing artist, he has passed on the influence to his son, Kyle, who is a professional jazz bassist and composer. An [audiophile](/wiki/Audiophile "Audiophile"), Eastwood owns an extensive collection of LPs which he plays on a Rockport [turntable](/wiki/Phonograph "Phonograph"). His favorite musicians include saxophonists [Charlie Parker](/wiki/Charlie_Parker "Charlie Parker") and [Lester Young](/wiki/Lester_Young "Lester Young"), pianists [Thelonious Monk](/wiki/Thelonious_Monk "Thelonious Monk"), [Oscar Peterson](/wiki/Oscar_Peterson "Oscar Peterson"), [Dave Brubeck](/wiki/Dave_Brubeck "Dave Brubeck"), and [Fats Waller](/wiki/Fats_Waller "Fats Waller"), and [Delta bluesman](/wiki/Delta_blues "Delta blues") [Robert Johnson](/wiki/Robert_Johnson "Robert Johnson").[306]

Eastwood has his own [Warner Bros. Records](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Records "Warner Bros. Records")-distributed imprint, Malpaso Records, as part of his deal with Warner Brothers. This deal was unchanged when [Warner Music Group](/wiki/Warner_Music_Group "Warner Music Group") was sold by Time Warner to private investors.[307] Malpaso Records, which has released all of the scores of Eastwood's films from _The Bridges of Madison County_ onward, has also released the album of a 1996 jazz concert he hosted, titled _Eastwood after Hours – Live at Carnegie Hall_. He composed the film scores of _Mystic River_ , _Million Dollar Baby_ , _Flags of Our Fathers_ , _[Grace Is Gone](/wiki/Grace_Is_Gone "Grace Is Gone")_ , _Changeling_ , _Hereafter_ , _J. Edgar_ , and the original piano compositions for _In the Line of Fire_. He co-wrote and performed the song heard over the credits of _Gran Torino_[308] and also co-wrote "Why Should I Care" with [Linda Thompson](/wiki/Linda_Thompson_\(actress\) "Linda Thompson \(actress\)") and [Carole Bayer Sager](/wiki/Carole_Bayer_Sager "Carole Bayer Sager"), a song recorded in 1999 by [Diana Krall](/wiki/Diana_Krall "Diana Krall").[307]

The music in _Grace Is Gone_ received two Golden Globe nominations by the [Hollywood Foreign Press Association](/wiki/Hollywood_Foreign_Press_Association "Hollywood Foreign Press Association") for the [65th Golden Globe Awards](/wiki/65th_Golden_Globe_Awards "65th Golden Globe Awards"). Eastwood was nominated for Best Original Score, while the song "Grace is Gone" with music by Eastwood and lyrics by Carole Bayer Sager was nominated for [Best Original Song](/wiki/Golden_Globe_Award_for_Best_Original_Song "Golden Globe Award for Best Original Song").[309] It won the [Satellite Award](/wiki/Satellite_Award "Satellite Award") for Best Song at the [12th Satellite Awards](/wiki/Satellite_Awards_2007 "Satellite Awards 2007"). _Changeling_ was nominated for Best Score at the 14th Critics' Choice Awards, Best Original Score at the 66th Golden Globe Awards, and Best Music at the 35th Saturn Awards. On September 22, 2007, Eastwood was awarded an honorary [Doctor of Music](/wiki/Doctor_of_Music "Doctor of Music") degree from the [Berklee College of Music](/wiki/Berklee_College_of_Music "Berklee College of Music") at the Monterey Jazz Festival, on which he serves as an active board member. Upon receiving the award he gave a speech claiming, "It's one of the great honors I'll cherish in this lifetime."[310]

The scoring stage at [Warner Bros. Studios, Burbank](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Studios_Burbank "Warner Bros. Studios Burbank") was renamed the Eastwood Scoring Stage in the 1990s.[311]

## Personal life

Verified children of Clint Eastwood

  * With an unidentified woman: 
    * Laurie (born 1954)[312]
  * With Roxanne Tunis: 
    * Kimber (born 1964)[312]
  * With Maggie Johnson: 
    * [Kyle](/wiki/Kyle_Eastwood "Kyle Eastwood") (born 1968)[312]
    * [Alison](/wiki/Alison_Eastwood "Alison Eastwood") (born 1972)[312]
  * With Jacelyn Reeves: 
    * [Scott](/wiki/Scott_Eastwood "Scott Eastwood") (born 1986)[312]
    * Kathryn (born 1988)[312]
  * With [Frances Fisher](/wiki/Frances_Fisher "Frances Fisher"): 
    * [Francesca](/wiki/Francesca_Eastwood "Francesca Eastwood") (born 1993)[312]
  * With [Dina Ruiz](/wiki/Dina_Ruiz "Dina Ruiz"): 
    * Morgan (born 1996)[312]



### Relationships and children

Main article: [Personal life of Clint Eastwood](/wiki/Personal_life_of_Clint_Eastwood "Personal life of Clint Eastwood")

Twice divorced, Eastwood has had numerous casual and serious relationships of varying length and intensity through his life, many of which overlapped. He has eight known children by six women,[312][313] only half of whom were contemporaneously acknowledged.[314][315] Eastwood refuses to confirm his exact number of offspring,[2] and media reports have widely differed on the total.[3] He avoids discussing his families with the press, stating, "they're vulnerable people. I can protect myself, but they can't."[316] His biographer, [Patrick McGilligan](/wiki/Patrick_McGilligan_\(biographer\) "Patrick McGilligan \(biographer\)"), has said on camera that Eastwood's total number of children is indeterminate and that "one was when he was still in high school".[317]

[](/wiki/File:Locke-Eastwood-1975.jpg)Locke and Eastwood in 1975 during the filming of _The Outlaw Josey Wales_

Eastwood's first marriage was to manufacturing secretary-turned-fitness instructor Margaret Neville Johnson in December 1953, after meeting her on a [blind date](/wiki/Blind_date "Blind date") the previous May.[318] During the courtship, he had an affair that resulted in his daughter Laurie (born 1954), who was [adopted](/wiki/Adopted "Adopted") by Clyde and Helen Warren of Seattle.[319][320][321][322][323] While the identity of Laurie's biological mother is not public record,[312] McGilligan stated that she belonged to a theater group Eastwood participated in.[d] Eastwood continued having affairs while married to Johnson, including a 1959–1973 relationship with stuntwoman Roxanne Tunis that produced a daughter, Kimber (born 1964).[325][326] Tunis and Eastwood maintained a "healthy relationship" until her death in 2023.[327]

Johnson tolerated the [open marriage](/wiki/Open_marriage "Open marriage") with Eastwood,[328][329] and the couple eventually had two children, [Kyle](/wiki/Kyle_Eastwood "Kyle Eastwood") (born 1968) and [Alison](/wiki/Alison_Eastwood "Alison Eastwood") (born 1972).[312] In 1975, Eastwood and married actress-director [Sondra Locke](/wiki/Sondra_Locke "Sondra Locke") began living together;[330] she had been in a [marriage of convenience](/wiki/Marriage_of_convenience "Marriage of convenience") since 1967 with [Gordon Leigh Anderson](/wiki/Gordon_Anderson_\(sculptor\) "Gordon Anderson \(sculptor\)"), an unemployed homosexual.[331][332][333][334] Locke claimed that Eastwood sang "She Made Me Monogamous" to her[326][335] and confided he had "never been in love before".[21][336] Fritz Manes, who had known Eastwood since junior high, described the relationship with Locke as "absolute total, blind love".[337] Eastwood finally divorced Johnson in 1984;[338] Locke, however, remained married to Anderson until her death in 2018.[339] According to Bill Brown, publisher of the _[Carmel Pine Cone](/wiki/Carmel_Pine_Cone "Carmel Pine Cone")_ , Eastwood considered Locke the love of his life,[340] yet he has never publicly addressed her death.[341]

In an unpublicized affair, Eastwood fathered two legally fatherless[342][343][344][345] children, [Scott](/wiki/Scott_Eastwood "Scott Eastwood") (born 1986) and Kathryn (born 1988) with flight attendant Jacelyn Reeves.[346] When Locke and Eastwood separated in 1989, she filed a [palimony](/wiki/Palimony_in_the_United_States "Palimony in the United States") lawsuit and later sued for fraud,[347] reaching settlements in both cases.[331] During the early to mid-1990s, Eastwood had a relationship with actress [Frances Fisher](/wiki/Frances_Fisher "Frances Fisher") that resulted in a daughter, [Francesca](/wiki/Francesca_Eastwood "Francesca Eastwood") (born 1993).[348] Eastwood married for the second time in 1996, to news anchor [Dina Ruiz](/wiki/Dina_Ruiz "Dina Ruiz"), who gave birth to their daughter Morgan that same year.[349] Ruiz and Eastwood's marriage lasted until 2014.[312]

Beginning in 2014, Eastwood was seen in the company of restaurant hostess Christina Sandera,[350] though neither publicly confirmed a romance.[351][352] Eastwood's spokespeople, managers, and press agents have long denied any knowledge of his personal life.[353][354] Sandera died of a heart attack in July 2024, at age 61.[355] By the fall of that year, Eastwood was in a new relationship, though his girlfriend has not been identified.[356][357][358]

### Health and leisure activities

[](/wiki/File:Hog_Breaths_Inn.jpg)The Hog's Breath Inn in Carmel, once owned by Eastwood

Eastwood has been a health and fitness fanatic since his teens. During the production of _[Rawhide](/wiki/Rawhide_\(TV_series\) "Rawhide \(TV series\)")_ , he appeared in magazines and journals that often documented his health-conscious lifestyle. In an August 1959 edition of _[TV Guide](/wiki/TV_Guide "TV Guide")_ , for example, Eastwood was photographed doing push-ups. He offered tips on fitness and nutrition, advising people to eat plenty of fruit and raw vegetables, take vitamins, and avoid sugar-loaded beverages, excessive alcohol, and overloading on carbohydrates.[359]

Eastwood's father's death from a heart attack at age 64 in 1970, described by Fritz Manes as "the only bad thing that ever happened to him in his life",[104] came as a shock to Eastwood, especially since his grandfather had lived to be 92. It had a profound impact on his life; from that point forward he became more productive, working with greater speed and efficiency on set, and adopted an even more rigorous health regimen.[105] Despite abstaining from hard liquor, he co-opened an old English-inspired pub called the Hog's Breath Inn in Carmel-by-the-Sea in 1971.[360] Eastwood eventually sold the pub in 1999 and now owns the [Mission Ranch Hotel and Restaurant](/wiki/Mission_Ranch "Mission Ranch"), also located in Carmel-by-the-Sea.[359][361][362][363]

[](/wiki/File:ClintEastwoodGolfing_\(cropped_B\).jpg)Playing golf at a charity fundraising event in 2015

Eastwood is an avid golfer and owns the [Tehàma Golf Club](/wiki/Teh%C3%A0ma_Golf_Club "Tehàma Golf Club"). He is an investor in the world-renowned [Pebble Beach Golf Links](/wiki/Pebble_Beach_Golf_Links "Pebble Beach Golf Links") west of Carmel and donates his time to charitable causes at major tournaments.[308][364][365] Eastwood is an FAA-licensed fixed wing and rotary craft private pilot and often flies his helicopter to the studios to avoid traffic.[366][367]

### Spiritual beliefs and meditation

In 1973, Eastwood told the film critic Gene Siskel, "No, I don't believe in God."[368] In 2023, his daughter Kathryn stated, "Most of my earthly family do not believe in or worship God. They either have a lack of faith or reject the god in the Bible in favor of other idols or ideas."[369] Eastwood has said that he finds spirituality in nature (as suggested by his Western, _Pale Rider_ , 1985), explaining that "I was born during the Depression and I was brought up with no specific church. We moved every four or five months during the first 14 years of my life, so I was sent to a different church depending on wherever we lived. Most of them were Protestant, but I went to other churches because my parents wanted me to try to figure out things for myself. They always said, 'I just want to expose you to some religious order and see if that's something you like'. So although my religious training was not really specific, I do feel spiritual things. If I stand on the side of the Grand Canyon and look down, it moves me in some way."[370]

He has also said: "It would be wonderful to talk with my parents again, who are, of course, deceased. It makes the idea of death much less scary. But then again, if you think that nothing happens after you die, maybe it makes you live life better. Maybe you're supposed to do the best you can by the gift you're given of life and that alone."[370]

In 1975, Eastwood publicly acknowledged his participation in [Transcendental Meditation](/wiki/Transcendental_Meditation "Transcendental Meditation") when he appeared on _[The Merv Griffin Show](/wiki/The_Merv_Griffin_Show "The Merv Griffin Show")_ with [Maharishi Mahesh Yogi](/wiki/Maharishi_Mahesh_Yogi "Maharishi Mahesh Yogi"), the founder of Transcendental Meditation.[361] He has meditated every morning for years.[371]

### Real estate interests

While serving in the US Army at nearby Fort Ord, Eastwood developed an interest in Carmel area real estate. Using income from his acting career, on December 24, 1967, he bought five parcels totaling 283 acres (115 ha) of land from Charles Sawyer along [Highway 1](/wiki/California_State_Route_1 "California State Route 1") near Malpaso Creek, south of the [Carmel Highlands](/wiki/Carmel_Highlands,_California "Carmel Highlands, California").[372]

In May 1968, Eastwood and actor [James Garner](/wiki/James_Garner "James Garner") bought 340 acres (138 ha) of wooded land in Carmel Valley from the Howard Hattan estate for $640,000. The property was located across Carmel Valley Road from the Rancho Cañada Country Club and golf course.[373] Eastwood and Garner donated the undeveloped land to the Housing Authority of the County of Monterey in November 1983, with the stipulation that part of it be used for senior housing.[374]

He named his production company Malpaso Productions.[375] Eastwood later bought another parcel in the Highlands, together totaling 650 acres (263 ha) across six parcels. In 1995, Monterey County purchased the Malpaso land from him for $3.08 million and placed a permanent conservation easement on the property.[376][377] Using the proceeds from the sale, Eastwood bought the 134 acres (54 ha) Odello Ranch at the mouth of the Carmel River that same year. He paid to lower the levees along the southern side of the Carmel River to protect the Mission Ranch resort he owned, as well as the neighboring Mission Fields residential area on the north side of the river, both of which were flooded in 1994.[376]

In 1997, Eastwood and his former wife Maggie Johnson, acting as the Eastwood Trust, donated 49 acres (20 ha) of the Odello Ranch property east of Highway 1 to the [Big Sur Land Trust](/wiki/Big_Sur_Land_Trust "Big Sur Land Trust"), along with the associated water rights.[378] On June 28, 2016, Eastwood donated the remaining Odello East land.[379] He also purchased 550 acres (223 ha), known as the Cañada Woods development, immediately east of the Odello Ranch.[376]

In 2010, at age 80, Eastwood spent approximately $20 million[380] to build a 15,949-square-foot (1,481.7 m2) compound in Carmel-by-the-Sea.[381][382] His California real estate portfolio also includes a 6,136-square-foot (570.1 m2) [Spanish-style](/wiki/Spanish_architecture "Spanish architecture") mansion in [Bel-Air](/wiki/Bel-Air,_Los_Angeles "Bel-Air, Los Angeles"),[383][384] the 1,067.5-acre (432.0 ha) [Rising River Ranch](/wiki/Rising_River_Ranch "Rising River Ranch") near [Cassel](/wiki/Cassel,_California "Cassel, California"),[385] an apartment in [Burbank](/wiki/Burbank,_California "Burbank, California"),[386] and a 5,575-square-foot (517.9 m2) [Desert modern](/wiki/Desert_modern "Desert modern") home in [La Quinta](/wiki/La_Quinta "La Quinta")[387] (sometimes misidentified as Palm Springs),[388] as well as a large but understated[389] house located next door to his longtime primary Bel-Air residence.[390][391] Eastwood is also known to have purchased property in two other states. He owns a 5,700-square-foot (530 m2) house in [Sun Valley, Idaho](/wiki/Sun_Valley,_Idaho "Sun Valley, Idaho"),[392][393] and a 1.13-acre, oceanfront manor in [Kihei, Hawaii](/wiki/Kihei,_Hawaii "Kihei, Hawaii").[394][395][396] The latter was featured in an episode of the 2012 reality show _[Mrs. Eastwood& Company](/wiki/Mrs._Eastwood_%26_Company "Mrs. Eastwood & Company")_.[397]

Eastwood previously lived in [Studio City](/wiki/Studio_City,_Los_Angeles "Studio City, Los Angeles"), [Sherman Oaks](/wiki/Sherman_Oaks,_Los_Angeles "Sherman Oaks, Los Angeles"), [Tiburon](/wiki/Tiburon,_California "Tiburon, California"), and [Pebble Beach](/wiki/Pebble_Beach,_California "Pebble Beach, California").[398]

## Filmography

Main article: [Clint Eastwood filmography](/wiki/Clint_Eastwood_filmography "Clint Eastwood filmography")

Eastwood has contributed to over 50 films during his career as actor, director, producer, and composer.[399] He has acted in several television series, including his co-starring role in _Rawhide_.[400] He started directing in 1971, and made his debut as a producer in 1982 with _Firefox_ , though he had been functioning as uncredited producer on all of his Malpaso Company films since _Hang 'Em High_ in 1968. Eastwood also has contributed music to his films, either through performing, writing, or composing. He has mainly starred in western, action, and drama films. According to the box office revenue tracking website [Box Office Mojo](/wiki/Box_Office_Mojo "Box Office Mojo"), films featuring Eastwood have grossed a total of more than $1.81 billion domestically, with an average of $38.6 million per film.[401]

## Awards and honors

Main article: [List of awards and nominations received by Clint Eastwood](/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Clint_Eastwood "List of awards and nominations received by Clint Eastwood")

[](/wiki/File:Clint_Eastwood_signature_at_Hollywood_Blvd.jpg)Eastwood's signature and handprints in Hollywood Boulevard

Eastwood has been recognized with multiple awards and nominations for his work in film, television, and music. His widest reception has been in film work, for which he has received Academy Awards, [Directors Guild of America Awards](/wiki/Directors_Guild_of_America_Award "Directors Guild of America Award"), Golden Globe Awards, and [People's Choice Awards](/wiki/People%27s_Choice_Awards "People's Choice Awards"), among others. Eastwood is one of only two people to have been twice nominated for Best Actor and Best Director for the same film (_Unforgiven_ and _Million Dollar Baby_) the other being [Warren Beatty](/wiki/Warren_Beatty "Warren Beatty") (_[Heaven Can Wait](/wiki/Heaven_Can_Wait_\(1978_film\) "Heaven Can Wait \(1978 film\)")_ and _[Reds](/wiki/Reds_\(film\) "Reds \(film\)")_).[402]

Along with Beatty, [Robert Redford](/wiki/Robert_Redford "Robert Redford"), [Richard Attenborough](/wiki/Richard_Attenborough "Richard Attenborough"), [Kevin Costner](/wiki/Kevin_Costner "Kevin Costner"), and [Mel Gibson](/wiki/Mel_Gibson "Mel Gibson"), he is one of the few directors who is best known as an actor but also has won an Academy Award for directing. On February 27, 2005, he became one of only three living directors, along with [Miloš Forman](/wiki/Milo%C5%A1_Forman "Miloš Forman") and Francis Ford Coppola, to have directed two Best Picture winners.[402] At the age of 74, he was the oldest recipient of the Academy Award for Best Director to date. Eastwood has directed five actors in Academy Award–winning performances: Gene Hackman in _Unforgiven_ , Tim Robbins and Sean Penn in _Mystic River_ , and Morgan Freeman and Hilary Swank in _Million Dollar Baby_. 

On August 21, 1984, Eastwood was honored at a ceremony at [Grauman's Chinese Theatre](/wiki/Grauman%27s_Chinese_Theatre "Grauman's Chinese Theatre") to record his hand and footprints in cement. Eastwood received the AFI Life Achievement Award in 1996, and received an honorary degree from AFI in 2009. On December 6, 2006, California Governor [Arnold Schwarzenegger](/wiki/Arnold_Schwarzenegger "Arnold Schwarzenegger") and First Lady [Maria Shriver](/wiki/Maria_Shriver "Maria Shriver") inducted Eastwood into the [California Hall of Fame](/wiki/California_Hall_of_Fame "California Hall of Fame") located at [The California Museum for History, Women, and the Arts](/wiki/The_California_Museum_for_History,_Women,_and_the_Arts "The California Museum for History, Women, and the Arts").[403]

In 2007, Eastwood was presented with the highest civilian distinction in France, [Légion d'honneur](/wiki/L%C3%A9gion_d%27honneur "Légion d'honneur"), at a ceremony in Paris. French President [Jacques Chirac](/wiki/Jacques_Chirac "Jacques Chirac") told Eastwood that he embodied "the best of Hollywood".[404] In October 2009, he was honored with the [Lumière Award](/wiki/Lumi%C3%A8re_Award_\(film_festival_award\) "Lumière Award \(film festival award\)") (in honor of the [Lumière Brothers](/wiki/Auguste_and_Louis_Lumi%C3%A8re "Auguste and Louis Lumière"), inventors of the [Cinematograph](/wiki/Cinematograph "Cinematograph")) at the inaugural [Lumière Festival](/wiki/Lumi%C3%A8re_Festival "Lumière Festival") in [Lyon](/wiki/Lyon "Lyon"), France. This award honors his entire career and his major contribution to the 7th Art. In February 2010, Eastwood was recognized by President Barack Obama with an arts and humanities award. Obama described Eastwood's films as "essays in individuality, hard truths and the essence of what it means to be American".[405]

Eastwood has also been awarded at least three honorary degrees from universities and colleges, including an honorary degree from the [University of the Pacific](/wiki/University_of_the_Pacific_\(United_States\) "University of the Pacific \(United States\)") in 2006, an honorary Doctor of Humane Letters from the [University of Southern California](/wiki/University_of_Southern_California "University of Southern California") on May 27, 2007, and an honorary Doctor of Music degree from the Berklee College of Music at the Monterey Jazz Festival on September 22, 2007.[406][407]

On February 26, 2009, Eastwood received the Cannes Film Festival's Honorary [Golden Palm](/wiki/Golden_Palm "Golden Palm") Award at a private ceremony in Paris. In the same year on July 22, he was honored by [Emperor Akihito of Japan](/wiki/Emperor_Akihito_of_Japan "Emperor Akihito of Japan") with the [Order of the Rising Sun](/wiki/Order_of_the_Rising_Sun "Order of the Rising Sun"), 3rd class, Gold Rays with Neck Ribbon for his contributions to the enhancement of [Japan–United States relations](/wiki/Japan%E2%80%93United_States_relations "Japan–United States relations").[408]

Eastwood won the Golden Pine lifetime achievement award at the 2013 International Samobor Film Music Festival, along with [Ryuichi Sakamoto](/wiki/Ryuichi_Sakamoto "Ryuichi Sakamoto") and [Gerald Fried](/wiki/Gerald_Fried "Gerald Fried").[409]

## Notes

  1. **^** Eastwood refuses to confirm how many children he has,[2] and there have been conflicting reports in the media regarding the number.[3]
  2. **^** The birth name of his mother has been misspelled in countless references. Ruth's birth name was Margret, not Margaret.[7] This according to state birth records.[8] It was never Margaret, as has often been said.
  3. **^** Locke's age was misstated in 50 years' worth of publications, including every Eastwood biography on the market during her lifetime.[127] At no point during their post-split mutual disparagements did the discrepancy come up, and it was not until after Locke's death that the press consistently acknowledged she was born in 1944.[128][129][130] If Eastwood knew Locke was older than she always pretended to be, he never let on, and allowed her lie to go uncorrected for decades.
  4. **^** In a December 2018 interview, Eastwood's grandson Lowell Thomas Murray IV said his yet-to-be-identified maternal grandmother "never told Eastwood she was pregnant or spoke to him again. It was clear he had no idea, so to make him look like a bad guy is inaccurate."[324] This notion is expressly denied by Patrick McGilligan, who insists Eastwood knew full well that he got a woman from Washington state pregnant and—according to McGilligan's "impeccable" sources—had told friends he suspected he might have a child there.[324]
